# 06-security-key-management.md

```markdown
# Security and Key Management

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the policies, controls, lifecycle rules, and operational procedures for cryptographic keys and secrets used by PrivateMesh / النسر. Its goal is to keep secret material protected throughout generation, storage, use, rotation, backup, recovery, and destruction.

## 2. Scope
This document applies to:
- Device identity keys.
- Session keys.
- Signing keys.
- Encryption keys.
- Recovery material.
- Trust anchors where applicable.
- Any secret used by the application or its supporting tools.

## 3. Key Management Principles
- Generate keys locally whenever possible.
- Protect keys at rest and in memory.
- Never store private keys in plaintext.
- Never log secret material.
- Restrict access to approved processes and roles.
- Rotate keys when required.
- Revoke compromised keys immediately.
- Document every key type and location.
- Keep the design reviewable and auditable.

## 4. Key Lifecycle
### 4.1 Generation
Keys shall be generated using approved cryptographic libraries within the protected local environment. Random values required for key generation shall come from a trusted cryptographic source.

### 4.2 Registration
When a key must be known to peers, only the public portion or a safe representation of the identity material may be shared.

### 4.3 Storage
Private keys shall be stored only in protected storage. If persistent storage is used, it must be encrypted and access controlled.

### 4.4 Use
Keys shall be used only for their intended purpose. The application should avoid exposing secret material to ordinary business logic.

### 4.5 Rotation
Keys shall be rotated on schedule or after any event that increases risk, such as compromise or suspected compromise.

### 4.6 Revocation
Compromised or retired keys shall be revoked and removed from active trust use.

### 4.7 Backup
Backups of key material must be encrypted, access controlled, and limited to approved recovery workflows.

### 4.8 Recovery
Recovery procedures must be documented, reviewed, and tested.

### 4.9 Destruction
Expired or revoked secrets must be destroyed securely so they cannot be reused.

## 5. Key Types
### 5.1 Identity Keys
Used to represent a device identity across secure communication flows.

### 5.2 Session Keys
Used for short-lived authenticated encrypted sessions.

### 5.3 Signing Keys
Used to verify message authenticity or signing-related protocol elements where applicable.

### 5.4 Encryption Keys
Used to protect content at rest or in transit depending on the component.

### 5.5 Recovery Keys
Used only for controlled restoration workflows, if such a mechanism is adopted.

## 6. Storage Requirements
- Keys must never be left in plaintext files.
- Keys should be isolated from normal application data when possible.
- Access to storage must be authenticated and authorized.
- Storage integrity must be verified.
- Backup locations must be documented.
- Storage paths and policies must be reviewed in the Key Management Plan.

## 7. Access Control
Only explicitly authorized components and roles may access key material. Access must be limited by purpose, environment, and time as much as possible.

## 8. Approved Operations
- Generate.
- Read protected key references.
- Use for approved cryptographic operations.
- Rotate.
- Revoke.
- Backup under policy.
- Restore under policy.
- Destroy.

## 9. Prohibited Operations
- Plaintext export without authorization.
- Logging secret data.
- Copying keys into general-purpose logs or analytics systems.
- AI access to raw private keys.
- Sharing keys across environments.
- Using keys outside their intended purpose.

## 10. Compromise Handling
If a key is suspected to be compromised:
1. Isolate the affected device or service.
2. Revoke the key.
3. Rotate dependent keys if needed.
4. Re-establish trust through the approved workflow.
5. Record the incident and update the risk register.

## 11. Documentation Requirements
The key management plan must record:
- Key type.
- Purpose.
- Owner.
- Storage location.
- Rotation policy.
- Revocation policy.
- Backup policy.
- Recovery procedure.
- Destruction procedure.

## 12. Responsibilities
### 12.1 Key Owner
Responsible for approval and lifecycle oversight.

### 12.2 Security Reviewer
Responsible for control review and approval of sensitive changes.

### 12.3 Operations Owner
Responsible for executing operational procedures safely.

### 12.4 Documentation Owner
Responsible for keeping this document synchronized with implementation.

## 13. Review Cycle
This document should be reviewed:
- Before major release.
- After any key compromise event.
- After any change to storage or recovery design.
- At least periodically as part of governance review.

## 14. Summary
Cryptographic keys are the core trust material of the system. Protecting them requires disciplined lifecycle control, restricted access, tested recovery, and explicit documentation.
```

# 07-protocol-spec.md

```markdown
# Protocol Specification

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the protocol behavior for device identity exchange, session establishment, message delivery, verification, rekeying, and error handling.

## 2. Scope
This specification applies to:
- Identity exchange.
- Handshake negotiation.
- Session creation.
- Message transmission.
- Session renewal.
- Session revocation.
- Replay protection.
- Version negotiation.
- Relay encapsulation when applicable.

## 3. Protocol Goals
- Provide authenticated communication.
- Protect confidentiality and integrity.
- Prevent replay and downgrade.
- Keep message parsing deterministic.
- Support future evolution through versioning.

## 4. Versioning Rules
- Every message shall contain a protocol version field.
- Peers must verify compatibility before continuing.
- Unsupported versions shall be rejected.
- Downgrade attempts shall be treated as security-relevant failures unless explicitly approved.

## 5. Message Structure
A message should include:
- Version.
- Message type.
- Sender ID.
- Receiver ID.
- Session ID.
- Timestamp.
- Nonce.
- Sequence number where required.
- Payload.
- Signature or authentication tag.
- Integrity metadata.

## 6. Message Types
- Identity announcement.
- Session hello.
- Challenge.
- Response.
- Verification result.
- Encrypted data message.
- Acknowledgment.
- Retry.
- Error.
- Rekey.
- Revocation notice.
- Relay envelope.

## 7. Session States
### 7.1 Init
Initial state before handshake begins.

### 7.2 Hello
One peer proposes a secure session.

### 7.3 Challenge
A challenge is issued to validate authenticity.

### 7.4 Verified
The peer identity has been accepted.

### 7.5 Active
Encrypted session traffic is allowed.

### 7.6 Rekey
The session is being refreshed or rotated.

### 7.7 Revoked
The session is no longer trusted.

### 7.8 Error
The session cannot proceed safely.

## 8. Handshake Flow
1. Peers exchange identity material.
2. Version compatibility is checked.
3. Challenge and response are exchanged.
4. Authentication is validated.
5. Session keys are derived.
6. The session becomes active.

## 9. Message Protection
- Confidentiality must be enforced for payloads.
- Integrity must be verified before delivery.
- Authenticity must be tied to the session or identity context.
- Freshness must be enforced using nonces, timestamps, or sequence control.
- Sensitive metadata should be minimized where possible.

## 10. Replay Protection
A message shall be rejected if it:
- Reuses a nonce incorrectly.
- Has an invalid or expired freshness value.
- Replays an already used sequence state.
- Falls outside session validity.

## 11. Error Handling
The protocol should define stable failure results for:
- Unsupported version.
- Authentication failure.
- Integrity failure.
- Replay detected.
- Session expired.
- Revoked identity.
- Policy denied.
- Relay failure.

## 12. Relay Envelope
When traffic passes through a relay:
- The relay may forward encrypted data only.
- The relay shall not need plaintext.
- Relay metadata should be minimized.
- Relay behavior must not change protocol trust semantics.

## 13. Rekeying
Sessions must support renewal or rekeying without exposing secret state unnecessarily. Rekeying should occur when:
- A session lifetime limit is reached.
- Security policy requires it.
- Risk level changes.
- A compromise is suspected.

## 14. Backward Compatibility
Backward compatibility is allowed only if explicitly documented and approved. Unsafe fallback behavior should be rejected by default.

## 15. Security Considerations
- Never trust transport by itself.
- Never assume relay trust.
- Never accept malformed or ambiguous structures.
- Never silently downgrade security.
- Never leak secret material through error messages.

## 16. Summary
The protocol is designed to be explicit, versioned, authenticated, and resistant to replay and downgrade attacks. Any change to this specification must be reflected in the architecture and traceability documents.
```

# 08-discovery-relay.md

```markdown
# Discovery and Relay

## 1. Purpose
This document defines how peers are discovered, how direct communication is established, and how messages are relayed when direct delivery is unavailable.

## 2. Scope
This document covers:
- Peer discovery.
- Bootstrap behavior.
- Reachability checks.
- Relay selection.
- Relay forwarding.
- Retry policy.
- Fallback behavior.
- Policy enforcement.

## 3. Discovery Principles
- Discovery must follow policy.
- Discovery should expose minimal metadata.
- Discovery should be bounded and predictable.
- Discovery results must be validated before use.

## 4. Discovery Modes
### 4.1 Local Discovery
Find peers within a local or nearby environment when allowed.

### 4.2 Policy-Based Discovery
Allow discovery only under approved rules.

### 4.3 Capability-Based Discovery
Use stable capability descriptors where appropriate.

### 4.4 Relay-Assisted Discovery
Use a relay to assist with discovery metadata when direct discovery is not sufficient.

## 5. Relay Principles
- Relays are not trusted with plaintext.
- Relays should only forward encrypted data.
- Relay selection must be policy-driven.
- Relay failure should not expose content.
- Relay abuse must be rate-limited and observable.

## 6. Relay Flow
1. A direct path is attempted first when available.
2. If direct delivery is unavailable, the system selects an approved relay.
3. The encrypted payload is wrapped for forwarding.
4. The relay forwards the payload without reading it.
5. The recipient verifies and processes the message.

## 7. Relay Selection Criteria
- Policy compliance.
- Reachability.
- Latency.
- Health status.
- Trust constraints.
- Abuse signals.
- Availability.

## 8. Retry Policy
- Retry attempts must be bounded.
- Retry behavior must be observable.
- Retry must not create infinite loops.
- Retry should respect message priority and freshness.

## 9. Fallback Modes
- Direct send.
- Relay send.
- Deferred queue.
- Local-only mode if no route is available.

## 10. Security Considerations
- Do not expose plaintext to discovery or relay layers.
- Minimize metadata.
- Validate any relay-provided information.
- Treat unavailable or suspicious relays as untrusted.
- Log abnormal patterns without leaking sensitive data.

## 11. Operational Considerations
- Relay health should be monitored.
- Discovery failures should be tracked.
- Policy changes should be versioned.
- Failures should produce actionable diagnostics.

## 12. Summary
Discovery and relay expand reach while preserving the security boundary around message content. These layers must remain subordinate to the secure core.
```

# تابع

أكيد. سأكمل الآن **بقية المستندات النهائية**: **mesh routing، طبقة AI، خطة الإصدارات، الاختبارات والجودة، التشغيل، والحوكمة** بصيغة تفصيلية ومكتملة وجاهزة للنسخ إلى Markdown [^90_1][^90_2][^90_3].\
حرصت هنا على إبقاء mesh لاحقًا على النواة الآمنة، وعزل AI بحدود صارمة، وربط الإطلاق بالتحقق، لأن هذه هي النقاط التي تمنع انزلاق المشروع إلى تعقيد أو مخاطرة غير مضبوطة [^90_4][^90_5][^90_3].

# 09-mesh-routing.md

```markdown
# Mesh Routing

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the design and operating rules for multi-hop routing in later releases of the system.

## 2. Scope
Mesh routing includes:
- Node-to-node forwarding.
- Route discovery.
- Path validation.
- Route repair.
- Loop prevention.
- Message forwarding across trusted policy boundaries.

## 3. Design Intent
Mesh routing is not part of the initial secure core. It is introduced only after identity, sessions, encrypted messaging, secure storage, and relay behavior are stable.

## 4. Mesh Principles
- Do not expose plaintext to intermediate nodes.
- Keep routing decisions auditable.
- Prefer stable and policy-compliant paths.
- Repair routes when links fail.
- Prevent loops and route oscillation.
- Minimize metadata leakage.

## 5. Routing Model
### 5.1 Node
A node is any device participating in forwarding or receiving approved traffic.

### 5.2 Link
A link is a communication relationship between two nodes.

### 5.3 Path
A path is a sequence of links used to deliver a message.

### 5.4 Hop
A hop is one forwarding step along a path.

## 6. Routing Lifecycle
1. Discover peers.
2. Validate eligible nodes.
3. Build candidate paths.
4. Select a path using policy.
5. Forward encrypted traffic.
6. Monitor path health.
7. Repair or replace broken routes.

## 7. Route Selection Criteria
- Policy compliance.
- Health status.
- Latency.
- Trust profile.
- Path length.
- Availability.
- Stability.

## 8. Route Repair
When a route fails, the system should:
- Detect the failure.
- Preserve message confidentiality.
- Select a safe alternate path.
- Avoid routing loops.
- Record the event for observability.

## 9. Security Considerations
- Intermediate nodes must not read content.
- Route metadata should be minimized.
- Suspicious nodes should be rate-limited or avoided.
- Route changes must be observable.
- Mesh behavior must not weaken session security.

## 10. Operational Considerations
- Mesh should be enabled only when the release target requires it.
- Routing metrics should be monitored.
- Mesh should be tested under failure and abuse scenarios.
- Fallback to relay or direct behavior should remain available where possible.

## 11. Summary
Mesh routing expands the system’s reach, but it also expands complexity and risk. For that reason, it must be introduced carefully and only after the secure messaging foundation is proven stable.
```

# 10-ai-layer.md

```markdown
# AI Layer

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the allowed and prohibited uses of AI within the PrivateMesh / النسر project.

## 2. Design Goal
AI must remain a bounded assistant. It may support summarization, classification, and anomaly hints, but it must never become an authority for secrets, identity, or security decisions.

## 3. Allowed Uses
- Summarize non-sensitive documentation.
- Classify operational events.
- Suggest possible anomalies.
- Assist with drafting text.
- Help maintain documentation consistency.
- Generate non-authoritative explanations.

## 4. Prohibited Uses
- Manage private keys.
- Approve security-sensitive changes.
- Make final identity or trust decisions.
- Read raw sensitive data without redaction.
- Bypass human approval.
- Trigger destructive operations.
- Operate with unrestricted environment access.

## 5. Boundary Rules
### 5.1 Least Privilege
AI tools must receive only the minimal data and permissions required for the task.

### 5.2 Redaction
Sensitive content should be redacted before AI processing whenever possible.

### 5.3 Human Approval
Any action with security impact or operational risk must require human approval.

### 5.4 Environment Separation
AI tools must not blur the boundary between development, staging, and production.

### 5.5 Logging
AI actions must be logged in a way that supports audit and review without exposing secrets.

## 6. Safe AI Workflows
- Documentation drafting.
- Release note summarization.
- Non-sensitive log summarization.
- Anomaly flagging for human review.
- Requirements wording assistance.

## 7. Unsafe AI Workflows
- Secret handling.
- Direct deployment commands.
- Key rotation.
- Trust delegation.
- Unreviewed code changes to security logic.
- Data access beyond approved views.

## 8. Testing AI Safety
AI behavior should be tested using:
- Allowed-case tests.
- Adversarial prompt tests.
- Redaction tests.
- Environment isolation tests.
- Human review of action logs.

## 9. Policy Enforcement
The system should enforce allowlists, deny-lists, and explicit approvals for AI-powered operations.

## 10. Summary
AI is useful only when tightly bounded. In this project, it is a helper, not a controller.
```

# 11-release-plan.md

```markdown
# Release Plan

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the staged delivery plan for the system and the conditions required to move between releases.

## 2. Release Strategy
The project should be delivered in controlled phases:
- 0.1 Proof of concept.
- 1.0 Secure core.
- 1.1 Stability hardening.
- 2.0 Discovery and relay.
- 2.1 Files and multi-device support.
- 3.0 Mesh routing.
- 3.1+ Optimization and governance.

## 3. Release Objectives
Each release should:
- Deliver a coherent set of capabilities.
- Preserve security and traceability.
- Improve operational maturity.
- Avoid unnecessary feature coupling.

## 4. Release Gates
A release may advance only if:
- Requirements are satisfied.
- Tests are passing.
- Security review is complete.
- Risks are understood and accepted.
- Rollback is available.
- Documentation is updated.
- ADRs are current.

## 5. Version Intent
### 5.1 0.1
Validate architecture and identity direction.

### 5.2 1.0
Deliver secure identity, sessions, encrypted messaging, and local storage.

### 5.3 1.1
Improve reliability, observability, and error handling.

### 5.4 2.0
Add discovery and relay behavior.

### 5.5 2.1
Add file transfer and multi-device support.

### 5.6 3.0
Add mesh routing.

### 5.7 3.1+
Add optimization, refinement, and governance enhancements.

## 6. Rollout Model
- Internal alpha.
- Controlled beta.
- Staged rollout.
- Full rollout after validation.

## 7. Rollback Model
Every release must include:
- A tested rollback plan.
- A backup and restore path.
- A decision owner.
- A communication path for incident handling.

## 8. Release Notes
Release notes must include:
- Version number.
- Added features.
- Fixed issues.
- Security changes.
- Breaking changes.
- Upgrade steps.
- Rollback steps.
- Known limitations.

## 9. Summary
The release plan exists to make progress visible while preventing unsafe promotion of unfinished or unverified work.
```

# 12-testing-quality.md

```markdown
# Testing and Quality

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the testing strategy and quality criteria for the project.

## 2. Test Strategy
The system should be validated using multiple test types:
- Unit testing.
- Integration testing.
- Security testing.
- Fuzz testing.
- Regression testing.
- Performance testing.
- Compatibility testing.
- Acceptance testing.

## 3. Quality Principles
- Every security-critical behavior must be tested.
- Tests must be traceable to requirements.
- Test failures must be actionable.
- Regression coverage should protect core behavior.
- Evidence should be preserved.

## 4. Test Categories
### 4.1 Unit Tests
Verify isolated functions and modules.

### 4.2 Integration Tests
Verify interactions between layers and components.

### 4.3 Security Tests
Verify authentication, encryption, replay resistance, and downgrade protection.

### 4.4 Fuzz Tests
Exercise parsers, codecs, and protocol handlers with unexpected inputs.

### 4.5 Regression Tests
Ensure previously fixed issues do not return.

### 4.6 Performance Tests
Measure latency, throughput, and resource usage.

### 4.7 Compatibility Tests
Check expected behavior across supported versions and environments.

### 4.8 Acceptance Tests
Confirm that each release meets its intended goals.

## 5. Quality Gates
A build or release should not pass unless:
- Required tests have succeeded.
- Critical security paths are covered.
- Known severe defects are absent.
- Traceability is complete for the release scope.

## 6. Evidence
Testing evidence should include:
- Test case IDs.
- Expected result.
- Actual result.
- Execution date.
- Environment.
- Owner.

## 7. Summary
Testing is part of design, not a final formality. The quality system must support safe delivery and safe recovery.
```

# 13-operations-runbook.md

```markdown
# Operations Runbook

## 1. Purpose
This runbook defines how to operate, monitor, update, and recover the system safely.

## 2. Operational Principles
- Prefer repeatable procedures.
- Avoid ad hoc actions in production.
- Log major operational changes.
- Preserve evidence during incidents.
- Keep rollback available.

## 3. Common Procedures
### 3.1 Start Service
1. Verify environment readiness.
2. Verify configuration.
3. Start the service.
4. Confirm health checks.
5. Confirm logs and metrics are normal.

### 3.2 Stop Service
1. Notify stakeholders if required.
2. Stop incoming traffic.
3. Stop the service.
4. Confirm shutdown.

### 3.3 Deploy Update
1. Review release checklist.
2. Confirm backup.
3. Deploy the new version.
4. Run smoke tests.
5. Monitor behavior.

### 3.4 Roll Back
1. Stop new traffic.
2. Restore the previous version.
3. Verify health.
4. Confirm stability.

### 3.5 Restore Backup
1. Validate backup integrity.
2. Restore data.
3. Verify state.
4. Resume service.

### 3.6 Rotate Keys
1. Generate new key material.
2. Update trust references.
3. Reauthenticate peers.
4. Revoke old material.
5. Verify continuity.

## 4. Incident Handling
### 4.1 Containment
Stop spread or exposure.

### 4.2 Evidence Preservation
Preserve logs, traces, and relevant records.

### 4.3 Notification
Inform the correct owner and escalation path.

### 4.4 Recovery
Restore service or security posture safely.

### 4.5 Review
Document cause, impact, and corrective actions.

## 5. Monitoring
Watch for:
- Health failures.
- Authentication failures.
- Relay abuse.
- Routing anomalies.
- Storage corruption.
- Resource exhaustion.
- Security alerts.

## 6. Logging
- Logs should be structured where possible.
- Sensitive content should not appear in logs.
- Log retention should follow policy.
- Access to logs must be controlled.

## 7. Contacts
- Operations Owner.
- Release Manager.
- Security Lead.
- Documentation Owner.

## 8. Summary
The runbook exists to turn operations into a controlled process rather than a series of improvisations.
```

# 14-governance-change.md

```markdown
# Governance and Change Control

## 1. Purpose
This document defines how changes are reviewed, approved, recorded, and communicated.

## 2. Governance Principles
- Major decisions must be documented.
- Security-sensitive changes need review.
- Release promotion must be controlled.
- Documentation must stay in sync with implementation.
- Human approval is required for risky changes.

## 3. Roles
### 3.1 Project Owner
Owns scope, priorities, and final business direction.

### 3.2 Technical Lead
Owns technical coherence and architecture alignment.

### 3.3 Security Lead
Owns security review and threat-sensitive decisions.

### 3.4 Operations Lead
Owns deployment and operational safety.

### 3.5 Documentation Owner
Owns document quality and consistency.

### 3.6 QA Lead
Owns test completeness and quality evidence.

### 3.7 AI Safety Reviewer
Owns review of AI boundaries and AI-related risk.

## 4. Change Control
A change that affects any of the following must be reviewed and recorded:
- Architecture.
- Threat model.
- Key handling.
- Protocol behavior.
- Release scope.
- AI permissions.
- Operational behavior.

## 5. Required Artifacts
- Updated requirement.
- Updated use case if needed.
- Updated architecture or threat model.
- New or updated ADR.
- Updated RTM.
- Updated tests.
- Updated release notes.
- Updated risk register entry.

## 6. Approval Model
No critical change should move forward without:
- Documented rationale.
- Assigned owner.
- Security review where relevant.
- Quality review where relevant.
- Release review where relevant.

## 7. Summary
Governance prevents drift. It ensures that the project remains explainable, reviewable, and safe as it grows.
```

# نعم تابع تغطية باقي الملفات والملحقات التقنية المتبقية

أكيد. فيما يلي **باقي الملفات والملحقات التقنية المتبقية** بصيغة تفصيلية ومكتملة، مع الحفاظ على نفس النسق والملاءمة للنسخ المباشر إلى Markdown [^91_1][^91_2][^91_3].\
ركّزت هنا على أن تكون مصفوفة التتبع واضحة وقابلة للمراجعة المستمرة، وسجل المخاطر بسيطًا ومملوءًا بالمالكين وخطط الاستجابة، لأن هذه هي أفضل ممارسات الاستخدام العملي طويل المدى [^91_4][^91_5][^91_6].

# 16-traceability-matrix.md

```markdown
# Requirements Traceability Matrix

## 1. Purpose
This document maps requirements to use cases, design components, ADRs, test cases, and release targets. It is used to ensure that every requirement is covered, verified, and reviewable.

## 2. Traceability Principles
- Every requirement must have a unique ID.
- Every requirement must map to at least one use case.
- Every requirement must map to at least one design element.
- Every requirement must map to at least one test case.
- The matrix must be maintained continuously throughout the project lifecycle.
- Both forward and backward traceability should be preserved.

## 3. Column Definitions
- **Req-ID**: Unique requirement identifier.
- **Requirement**: The requirement statement.
- **Source**: Where the requirement came from.
- **Use Case**: Related use case(s).
- **Design Component**: Related system component(s).
- **ADR-ID**: Related architecture decision record.
- **Test Case IDs**: Verification cases.
- **Evidence**: Link or reference to proof.
- **Release**: Target release.
- **Status**: Current state.

## 4. Traceability Table
| Req-ID | Requirement | Source | Use Case | Design Component | ADR-ID | Test Case IDs | Evidence | Release | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FR-001 | Generate a unique local identity for each device | 01-vision-scope / 02-requirements | UC-001 | Identity Manager | ADR-0001 | TC-001 | Pending | 0.1 | Active |
| FR-002 | Generate cryptographic keys locally on the device | 02-requirements | UC-001 | Key Manager | ADR-0001 | TC-002 | Pending | 0.1 | Active |
| FR-003 | Establish an authenticated secure session between peers | 02-requirements | UC-002 | Session Manager | ADR-0002 | TC-010 | Pending | 1.0 | Active |
| FR-004 | Support session renewal and recovery after interruption | 02-requirements | UC-002 | Session Manager | ADR-0002 | TC-011 | Pending | 1.0 | Active |
| FR-005 | Encrypt messages end to end before transmission | 02-requirements | UC-003 | Message Codec | ADR-0002 | TC-020 | Pending | 1.0 | Active |
| FR-006 | Verify message integrity and authenticity before delivery | 02-requirements | UC-004 | Message Codec | ADR-0002 | TC-021 | Pending | 1.0 | Active |
| FR-007 | Store sensitive local data in encrypted form | 02-requirements | UC-001 / UC-004 | Secure Store | ADR-0004 | TC-030 | Pending | 1.0 | Active |
| FR-008 | Discover peers according to allowed policy | 02-requirements / 08-discovery-relay | UC-005 | Discovery Service | ADR-0005 | TC-040 | Pending | 2.0 | Planned |
| FR-009 | Relay messages without exposing plaintext content | 02-requirements / 08-discovery-relay | UC-006 | Relay Adapter | ADR-0005 | TC-041 | Pending | 2.0 | Planned |
| FR-010 | Support secure file transfer in chunks | 02-requirements | UC-007 | File Transfer Module | ADR-0006 | TC-050 | Pending | 2.1 | Planned |
| FR-011 | Allow one identity to be used across multiple authorized devices | 01-vision-scope / 02-requirements | UC-001 / UC-007 | Device Sync Manager | ADR-0006 | TC-051 | Pending | 2.1 | Planned |
| FR-012 | Support multi-hop routing in later releases | 02-requirements / 09-mesh-routing | UC-008 | Routing Engine | ADR-0005 | TC-080 | Pending | 3.0 | Planned |
| FR-013 | Provide limited AI-assisted support for documentation and anomaly hints | 01-vision-scope / 10-ai-layer | N/A | AI Assistant Layer | ADR-0007 | TC-090 | Pending | 3.1+ | Planned |
| NFR-001 | Protect confidentiality, integrity, and authentication | 02-requirements | All | All security layers | ADR-0002 | TC-100 | Pending | All | Active |
| NFR-002 | Minimize metadata exposure | 02-requirements | All | Transport / Storage / AI | ADR-0002 | TC-101 | Pending | All | Active |
| NFR-003 | Deliver acceptable message latency under expected load | 02-requirements | All | Transport / Relay / Routing | ADR-0008 | TC-102 | Pending | 1.0+ | Active |
| NFR-004 | Continue operating safely during partial failures | 02-requirements | All | Sessions / Storage / Ops | ADR-0008 | TC-103 | Pending | All | Active |
| NFR-005 | Support rollback, restore, and recovery procedures | 02-requirements / 13-operations-runbook | All | Operations | ADR-0009 | TC-104 | Pending | All | Active |
| NFR-006 | Be modular, documented, and supportable | 02-requirements | All | Architecture / Docs | ADR-0010 | TC-105 | Pending | All | Active |
| NFR-007 | Support unit, integration, security, fuzzing, and acceptance testing | 02-requirements / 12-testing-quality | All | QA Process | ADR-0010 | TC-106 | Pending | All | Active |
| NFR-008 | Maintain bidirectional traceability across artifacts | 02-requirements | All | Docs / RTM / ADR | ADR-0010 | TC-107 | Pending | All | Active |
```


## 5. Maintenance Rules

- Update the matrix whenever a requirement changes.
- Review it at each release gate.
- Validate that every active requirement has at least one test case.
- Remove or supersede obsolete entries deliberately rather than silently.
- Keep evidence references current and accessible.


## 6. Review Cadence

The matrix should be reviewed:

- Weekly during active development.
- At each release readiness review.
- After any architectural or protocol change.
- After any security incident.
- Before archiving a release.


## 7. Summary

The RTM is the control layer that connects intent to implementation and implementation to proof.

````

# 17-risk-register.md

```markdown
# Risk Register

## 1. Purpose
This document lists the known project risks, their owners, their severity, and their response plans. It is intended to remain active throughout the project lifecycle.

## 2. Risk Management Principles
- Keep risks clear and actionable.
- Assign every risk an owner.
- Score each risk by likelihood and impact.
- Review risks regularly.
- Record response actions and status changes.
- Link risks to releases and decisions where relevant.

## 3. Risk Scoring
A risk score may be derived from likelihood and impact using a simple qualitative scale.

## 4. Risk Table
| Risk-ID | Risk | Category | Likelihood | Impact | Score | Owner | Response | Status | Last Review |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| RSK-001 | Private key leakage from device compromise | Security | High | Critical | 25 | Security Lead | Mitigate: protected storage, revocation, recovery workflow | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-002 | Replay attack against handshake or message flow | Security | Medium | Critical | 20 | Crypto Owner | Mitigate: nonce, sequence checks, freshness rules | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-003 | Downgrade to weaker protocol behavior | Security / Architecture | Low | Critical | 15 | Architecture Lead | Avoid: strict version negotiation, reject unsafe fallback | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-004 | Malicious relay behavior or traffic analysis | Security / Network | Medium | High | 16 | Network Lead | Mitigate: minimize metadata, encrypt content, rate limit | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-005 | Local storage corruption causes message loss | Reliability | Medium | High | 16 | Storage Owner | Mitigate: integrity checks, journaling, restore path | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-006 | Incomplete traceability between requirements and tests | Process | Medium | High | 16 | Documentation Owner | Mitigate: maintain RTM and review at release gates | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-007 | Release failure with no safe rollback path | Operations | Medium | High | 16 | Release Manager | Mitigate: rollback checklist, staged rollout, validation | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-008 | AI layer exposes sensitive metadata or content | AI / Privacy | Medium | Critical | 20 | AI Safety Reviewer | Mitigate: redaction, local processing, approval controls | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-009 | Mesh routing complexity delays delivery of the secure core | Schedule / Architecture | Medium | High | 16 | Project Owner | Mitigate: defer mesh until core is stable | Open | 2026-09-28 |
| RSK-010 | Insufficient test coverage for security-critical paths | Quality | High | High | 20 | QA Lead | Mitigate: mandatory security and regression tests | Open | 2026-09-28 |
````


## 5. Risk Review Rules

- Review the register regularly, not only at milestones.
- Update risk status when mitigations change.
- Close risks only when the acceptance criteria are met.
- Escalate critical risks immediately.
- Add newly discovered risks promptly.


## 6. Risk Closure Criteria

A risk may be closed only when:

- The underlying issue has been mitigated or removed.
- The owner approves closure.
- The closure is recorded with evidence.


## 7. Summary

The risk register should remain simple, explicit, and owned. That keeps it useful instead of ornamental.

````

***

# 18-release-checklist.md

```markdown
# Release Checklist

## 1. Purpose
This checklist is used to confirm that a release is ready to proceed safely.

## 2. Pre-Release Checks
- [ ] All relevant documents are updated.
- [ ] The requirements traceability matrix is current.
- [ ] The risk register is reviewed.
- [ ] Required ADRs are approved.
- [ ] Test cases for the release scope have passed.
- [ ] Security tests have passed.
- [ ] Regression tests have passed.
- [ ] Performance checks are acceptable.
- [ ] Rollback plan is documented and tested.
- [ ] Backup and restore procedures are validated.
- [ ] Release notes are written.
- [ ] Monitoring and alerting are ready.
- [ ] Operational contacts are confirmed.
- [ ] Sign-off has been obtained.

## 3. Go Criteria
A release may proceed only if:
- No critical defects remain open.
- No critical security findings remain open.
- Traceability is complete for the release scope.
- Rollback is available and verified.
- Required approvals have been obtained.

## 4. No-Go Criteria
A release must stop if:
- A critical defect is open.
- A security issue is unresolved.
- Evidence is missing.
- Rollback is untested or unavailable.
- Required approvals are incomplete.

## 5. Post-Release Checks
- [ ] Service health is stable.
- [ ] Error rates are within expected limits.
- [ ] Logs and telemetry are accessible.
- [ ] Rollback remains available.
- [ ] Known issues are recorded.

## 6. Summary
The checklist is a release gate, not a formality. It protects the project from preventable operational and security failures.
````

# 00-charter.md

```markdown
# Project Charter

## 1. Project Name
PrivateMesh / النسر

## 2. Purpose
This project authorizes the design and implementation of a privacy-first, decentralized secure communication system. The system will begin with a secure core and expand in controlled releases.

## 3. Problem Statement
Many communication systems centralize trust, expose metadata, or weaken user control. This project addresses those gaps by providing secure local identity, authenticated sessions, encrypted messaging, and later relay and mesh capabilities.

## 4. Objectives
- Build local device identity generation.
- Establish authenticated secure sessions.
- Protect message content and integrity.
- Protect sensitive local storage.
- Support relay transport and later mesh routing.
- Maintain complete documentation and traceability.
- Use release gates to prevent unsafe promotion.

## 5. High-Level Scope
### In Scope
- Identity generation.
- Key management.
- Session establishment.
- Encrypted messaging.
- Secure storage.
- Peer discovery.
- Relay transport.
- File transfer.
- Multi-device support.
- Mesh routing in later releases.
- Testing, release planning, operations, governance.

### Out of Scope
- Centralized identity authority.
- Unrestricted AI control.
- Mesh routing before the secure core is stable.
- Consumer social-network features.
- Security decisions made by AI.

## 6. Success Criteria
- Secure core works in release 1.0.
- Requirements are traceable to tests and design.
- ADRs exist for major decisions.
- Rollback and recovery are documented.
- Security and quality gates prevent unsafe release.

## 7. Stakeholders
- Project Owner
- Technical Lead
- Security Lead
- Operations Lead
- Documentation Owner
- QA Lead
- AI Safety Reviewer

## 8. Constraints
- Private keys must remain locally controlled.
- Security must take priority over convenience.
- Every major decision must be documented.
- Releases must be staged and reviewable.

## 9. Governance
Any change that affects architecture, threat model, protocol, key management, or release behavior must be documented and reviewed.

## 10. Approval
This charter is effective after approval by the Project Owner and Technical Lead.
```


______________________________________________________________________

# 01-vision-scope.md

```markdown
# Vision and Scope

## 1. Vision
Create a secure, decentralized communication platform that starts with a strong encrypted core and grows into a resilient mesh-capable system.

## 2. Mission
Provide users with secure identity, authenticated communication, encrypted messaging, and controlled networking without relying on a central trust anchor.

## 3. Product Principles
- Privacy first.
- Security first.
- Local key control.
- Minimal metadata.
- Clear documentation.
- Incremental delivery.
- Testability.
- Change control.

## 4. Product Scope
### 4.1 Core Scope
- Device identity generation.
- Secure handshake.
- Session management.
- Encrypted messaging.
- Secure storage.
- Integrity verification.
- Release governance.
- Test and operations support.

### 4.2 Extended Scope
- Peer discovery.
- Relay-based delivery.
- File transfer.
- Multi-device support.
- Mesh routing.
- Monitoring and observability.
- Limited AI support.

## 5. Out of Scope
- Central identity service.
- Broad social platform features.
- Unrestricted AI access to sensitive content.
- Mesh routing before the secure core is stable.
- Security-critical decision-making by AI.

## 6. Release Stages
### 6.1 0.1
Proof of concept.

### 6.2 1.0
Secure core.

### 6.3 1.1
Stability hardening.

### 6.4 2.0
Discovery and relay.

### 6.5 2.1
Files and multi-device support.

### 6.6 3.0
Mesh routing.

### 6.7 3.1+
Optimization and governance improvements.

## 7. Scope Control
Scope changes must update requirements, RTM, risk register, ADRs, and release plan.

## 8. Success Measures
- Secure messaging works end to end.
- Key handling remains local and protected.
- Traceability stays complete.
- Release gates are enforced.
```

# 02-requirements.md

```markdown
# Software Requirements Specification

## 1. Purpose
This document specifies the functional, non-functional, security, and operational requirements for PrivateMesh / النسر. All requirements are intended to be verifiable and traceable.

## 2. Document Rules
- Use unique requirement IDs.
- Write requirements in clear, measurable language.
- Keep requirements testable.
- Include the source of each requirement.
- Link each requirement to use cases and tests.
- Record changes under version control.

## 3. Functional Requirements
### FR-001
The system shall generate a unique local identity for each device.

### FR-002
The system shall generate cryptographic keys locally on the device.

### FR-003
The system shall establish an authenticated secure session between peers.

### FR-004
The system shall support session renewal after interruption or expiry.

### FR-005
The system shall encrypt messages end to end before transmission.

### FR-006
The system shall verify message integrity and authenticity before delivery.

### FR-007
The system shall store sensitive local data in encrypted form.

### FR-008
The system shall discover peers according to allowed policy.

### FR-009
The system shall relay messages without exposing plaintext content.

### FR-010
The system shall support secure file transfer in chunks.

### FR-011
The system shall allow one identity to be used across multiple authorized devices.

### FR-012
The system shall support multi-hop routing in later releases.

### FR-013
The system shall provide limited AI-assisted support for documentation and anomaly hints.

## 4. Non-Functional Requirements
### NFR-001 Security
The system shall protect confidentiality, integrity, and authentication.

### NFR-002 Privacy
The system shall minimize metadata exposure.

### NFR-003 Performance
The system shall deliver acceptable message latency under expected load.

### NFR-004 Reliability
The system shall continue operating safely during partial failures.

### NFR-005 Recoverability
The system shall support rollback, restore, and recovery procedures.

### NFR-006 Maintainability
The system shall be modular, documented, and supportable.

### NFR-007 Testability
The system shall support unit, integration, security, fuzzing, and acceptance testing.

### NFR-008 Traceability
The system shall maintain bidirectional traceability across requirements, use cases, design, tests, and releases.

## 5. Security Requirements
### SR-001
Private keys shall not be stored in plaintext.

### SR-002
The system shall reject unauthenticated or tampered messages where authentication is required.

### SR-003
The system shall prevent replay attacks using freshness controls.

### SR-004
The system shall reject downgrade attempts unless explicitly approved.

### SR-005
The system shall support key rotation and key revocation.

### SR-006
The system shall protect logs from leaking sensitive data.

### SR-007
The AI layer shall not control key management or final security decisions.

## 6. Operational Requirements
### OR-001
The system shall provide deployment and rollback procedures.

### OR-002
The system shall provide monitoring and alerting.

### OR-003
The system shall provide restore procedures for backup data.

### OR-004
The system shall provide a documented incident response path.

## 7. Priority
- Must: identity, handshake, sessions, encrypted messaging, local storage, security controls.
- Should: discovery, relay, file transfer, multi-device support.
- Could: AI-assisted analysis and documentation.
- Won’t now: full mesh routing in the earliest release.

## 8. Verification
Each requirement shall be verified by test, inspection, analysis, or demonstration.

## 9. Traceability
Each requirement shall map to:
- source,
- use case,
- architecture component,
- ADR,
- test case,
- release version.

## 10. Acceptance
A requirement is accepted for a release only if it is implemented, tested, documented, and linked in the RTM.
```

# 15-adr/ADR-0001.md

```markdown
# ADR-0001: Generate device identity locally

## Status
Accepted

## Date
2026-09-28

## Context
The system requires a trustworthy device identity model without relying on a central identity authority.

## Decision
Device identity shall be generated locally on the device, and the private key shall remain under local protected storage.

## Alternatives
1. Central identity server.
2. Cloud identity service.
3. Local identity generation.

## Rationale
Local generation preserves decentralization, reduces trust in external infrastructure, and keeps private keys under device control.

## Consequences
### Positive
- Better privacy.
- Lower central dependency.
- Smaller trust boundary.

### Negative
- Recovery and revocation must be designed carefully.
- Device loss has greater impact.
```


______________________________________________________________________

# 15-adr/ADR-0002.md

```markdown
# ADR-0002: Use authenticated session keys for messaging

## Status
Accepted

## Date
2026-09-28

## Context
Messages require confidentiality and integrity without exposing long-term identity keys for every message.

## Decision
The system shall establish authenticated session keys during handshake and use them for message encryption and integrity protection.

## Alternatives
1. Encrypt every message with the identity key.
2. Use unauthenticated session encryption.
3. Use authenticated session keys.

## Rationale
Session keys reduce exposure of long-term keys and support renewal and revocation.

## Consequences
- More handshake complexity.
- Better security separation between identity and transport.
```


______________________________________________________________________

# 15-adr/ADR-0003.md

```markdown
# ADR-0003: Reject protocol downgrade by default

## Status
Accepted

## Date
2026-09-28

## Context
Attackers may try to force peers to fall back to weaker protocol behavior.

## Decision
The system shall reject protocol downgrade attempts by default and only allow explicitly approved compatibility paths.

## Alternatives
1. Silent fallback to older protocol versions.
2. Manual fallback only when explicitly approved.
3. Hard reject downgrade attempts by default.

## Rationale
This prevents security regression and simplifies auditability.

## Consequences
- Stronger security.
- Legacy peers may fail to connect unless compatibility is explicitly handled.
```


______________________________________________________________________

# 15-adr/ADR-0004.md

```markdown
# ADR-0004: Store private keys in protected local storage

## Status
Accepted

## Date
2026-09-28

## Context
Private keys are the most sensitive local asset and must not be exposed in plaintext.

## Decision
Private keys shall be stored only in protected local storage and never exposed in plaintext in logs, files, or protocol payloads.

## Alternatives
1. Plaintext storage.
2. Encrypted storage without access control.
3. Protected local storage with encryption and access control.

## Rationale
This minimizes key exposure and supports the security model.

## Consequences
- Reduced risk of direct compromise.
- More careful recovery design required.
```


______________________________________________________________________

# 15-adr/ADR-0005.md

```markdown
# ADR-0005: Defer mesh routing until the secure core is stable

## Status
Accepted

## Date
2026-09-28

## Context
Mesh routing adds complexity and risk.

## Decision
Mesh routing shall be deferred to a later release. Initial releases shall focus on secure core messaging and relay-based transport.

## Alternatives
1. Build mesh routing in the first release.
2. Build a limited mesh in parallel.
3. Defer mesh routing until the secure core is stable.

## Rationale
This reduces early complexity and focuses effort on the highest-risk security foundations.

## Consequences
- Secure core reaches users sooner.
- Mesh capability arrives later.
```

## ختام

بهذا تكون الحزمة مكتملة من حيث المستندات الأساسية والملحقات التقنية، ومعها الهيكل الذي يربط بين الميثاق، النطاق، المتطلبات، التصميم، التهديدات، التتبع، المخاطر، والإطلاق [^92_2][^92_4][^92_6].\
المرحلة التالية المنطقية هي توحيد أسماء المسارات داخل المستودع النهائي والتأكد من أن روابط `llms.txt` و`docs-index.md` تطابق البنية الفعلية للملفات [^92_7][^92_5].

# تابع

أكيد. سأعطيك الآن **النسخة الختامية الموحّدة** التي تُصلح ما تبقى من هيكلة المشروع، مع **ترتيب المستندات، تنظيف أسماء الملفات، وإغلاق الحزمة كمرجع واحد** [^93_1][^93_2][^93_3].\
الفكرة هنا هي تحويل كل شيء إلى **Single Source of Truth** داخل `docs/` مع تسلسل واضح من الشرح إلى المرجع إلى التتبع، لأن أفضل الممارسات تؤكد أهمية القالب الموحد، العناوين الواضحة، والتوثيق القابل للمسح السريع والربط الداخلي [^93_4][^93_5][^93_6].

### `docs-index.md`

اجعله مجرد فهرس قصير للقراءة السريعة:

- ما هو المستند.
- لماذا يقرأه الشخص.
- ما الذي يرتبط به.


## ملاحظات تنظيمية مهمة

- اجعل كل مستند يبدأ بـ **Purpose** ثم **Scope** ثم **Body** ثم **Summary**، لأن التوثيق المنظم يسهّل التصفح ويقلل الضياع [^93_2][^93_5][^93_6].
- استخدم عناوين قصيرة وواضحة، وقلل التكرار بين الملفات، مع إبقاء التفاصيل المرجعية في المستند المناسب بدل إعادة نسخها في أكثر من مكان [^93_4][^93_5].
- ضع المتطلبات عالية الأهمية في مقدمة `02-requirements.md` و`16-traceability-matrix.md`، لأن التتبع ثنائي الاتجاه ينبغي أن يبدأ مبكرًا ويستمر طوال عمر المشروع [^93_1][^93_7][^93_8].
- اربط كل قرار معماري ذي أثر بـ ADR منفصل، لأن سجل القرار هو ما يحفظ “لماذا” وليس فقط “ماذا” [^93_2][^93_1].


## النسخة النهائية المختصرة للملفات المتبقية

إذا أردت إغلاق الهيكل بشكل كامل ومرتب، استخدم هذا التوزيع:

- **Narrative docs**: `00` إلى `14`.
- **Decision docs**: `15-adr/`.
- **Traceability docs**: `16`, `17`, `18`.
- **Appendices**: glossary, abbreviations, failure scenarios, contacts, docs index.
- **Machine-readable entry point**: `llms.txt`.


## النتيجة

بهذا الشكل يصبح لديك:

- مستودع وثائقي منظم.
- تتبع واضح من المتطلب إلى الاختبار.
- قرارات معمارية محفوظة.
- مخاطر وإطلاق وتشغيل موثّق.
- فهرس مناسب للقراءة البشرية والآلية [^93_1][^93_3][^93_2].

إذا أردت، أكتب لك الآن **docs-index.md النهائي** و**llms.txt النهائي** بصياغة جاهزة للنسخ، مع روابط صحيحة ومطابقة لهذه البنية.

# نعم

أكيد. هذا هو **`llms.txt` النهائي** و\*\*`docs-index.md` النهائي\*\* بعد تنظيف البنية وربطها بشكل صحيح داخل `docs/` [^94_1][^94_2][^94_3].\
اتّبعت هنا القاعدة الأساسية: ملف `llms.txt` قصير، واضح، ويشير إلى ملفات Markdown الأساسية مباشرة، بينما `docs-index.md` يعمل كفهرس بشري سريع القراءة [^94_1][^94_4][^94_5].

## docs/llms.txt

```text
# PrivateMesh / النسر

> Secure decentralized communication system focused on identity, encrypted messaging, relay transport, and phased mesh networking.

## Core Documents
- [00-charter.md](./00-charter.md): Project purpose, goals, stakeholders, constraints, and governance.
- [01-vision-scope.md](./01-vision-scope.md): Vision, scope, principles, out-of-scope items, and release stages.
- [02-requirements.md](./02-requirements.md): Functional, non-functional, security, and operational requirements.
- [03-use-cases.md](./03-use-cases.md): Primary user and system scenarios.
- [04-architecture.md](./04-architecture.md): System layers, components, trust boundaries, and design principles.
- [05-threat-model.md](./05-threat-model.md): Assets, attackers, threats, mitigations, and residual risk.
- [06-security-key-management.md](./06-security-key-management.md): Key lifecycle, storage, rotation, revocation, backup, and recovery.
- [07-protocol-spec.md](./07-protocol-spec.md): Message format, session states, validation rules, and versioning.
- [08-discovery-relay.md](./08-discovery-relay.md): Discovery, relay selection, retry policy, and fallback behavior.
- [09-mesh-routing.md](./09-mesh-routing.md): Multi-hop routing, route repair, loop prevention, and mesh constraints.
- [10-ai-layer.md](./10-ai-layer.md): AI-assisted features, boundaries, allowed use, and safety limits.
- [11-release-plan.md](./11-release-plan.md): Version roadmap, release gates, rollout strategy, and rollback model.
- [12-testing-quality.md](./12-testing-quality.md): Test strategy, quality gates, evidence, and acceptance criteria.
- [13-operations-runbook.md](./13-operations-runbook.md): Deployment, monitoring, restore, incident handling, and maintenance.
- [14-governance-change.md](./14-governance-change.md): Roles, approvals, change control, and documentation governance.

## Traceability and Control
- [16-traceability-matrix.md](./16-traceability-matrix.md): Requirement-to-use-case-to-design-to-test mapping.
- [17-risk-register.md](./17-risk-register.md): Project risks, owners, treatments, and review status.
- [18-release-checklist.md](./18-release-checklist.md): Pre-release verification checklist.

## Decision Records

## Appendices
- [appendices/glossary.md](./appendices/glossary.md): Key terms and definitions.

- [appendices/failure-scenarios.md](./appendices/failure-scenarios.md): Expected failure and attack scenarios.
```

## ملاحظة أخيرة

بهذا أصبحت البنية متوافقة مع فكرة `llms.txt`: **ملف قصير، مرجعي، ويقود إلى Markdown نظيف وواضح**، مع فهرس بشري منفصل يساعد المراجعة والتنقل [^94_1][^94_6][^94_7].\
الخطوة التالية المنطقية فقط هي توحيد أسماء الملفات الفعلية في المستودع مع هذه الروابط ثم إنشاء `README.md` أعلى الجذر إن أردت صفحة بدء بشرية مختصرة [^94_8][^94_2][^94_9].

أكيد. سأعطيك الآن **حزمة عملية من ثلاث قطع**: `README.md` للجذر مرتبط بـ `llms.txt`، **مراجعة توافق** `07-protocol-spec.md` مع المعايير المقترحة، و**مسودات كود/هيكل عملي** لطبقة `10-ai-layer.md` [^95_1][^95_2][^95_3].\
سأبقيها مباشرة وقابلة للاستخدام، وأربط الملاحظات بمعيار `llms.txt`، وتعريف حالات البروتوكول، وحدود الأمان الخاصة بالـ AI [^95_1][^95_4][^95_3].

## مراجعة توافق 07-protocol-spec.md

### التقييم العام

الملف الحالي **متوافق بشكل جيد مبدئيًا** مع المعايير المقترحة، لكنه يحتاج بعض التحسينات ليكون أكثر قربًا من صياغة بروتوكول قوي ومحدد [^95_5][^95_2][^95_6].\
أهم نقطة تحسّن هي جعل **تبادل النسخة وحسمها** أكثر صراحة داخل handshake، لأن مواصفات secured messaging في SPDM تعرض النسخ المدعومة ثم يتم اختيار نسخة واحدة ثابتة للجلسة [^95_2][^95_7].

### ما هو جيد بالفعل

- وجود **Message Structure** واضح.
- وجود **Session States**.
- وجود **Replay Protection**.
- وجود **Versioning Rules**.
- وجود **Relay Envelope** منفصل عن المحتوى [^95_2][^95_7][^95_6].


### ما يحتاج تحسينًا

1. **إضافة قسم رسمي لـ Negotiation/Version Discovery**.\
يجب أن يذكر أن الطرفين يعلنان النسخ المدعومة ثم يتم تثبيت نسخة واحدة للجلسة، ولا تتغير بعد الاختيار [^95_2][^95_7].
2. **تعريف أوضح لـ state transitions**.\
من الأفضل إضافة جدول ينتقل من Init إلى Hello إلى Challenge إلى Verified إلى Active إلى Rekey/Revoked/Error، حتى يكون الفهم والتنفيذ متسقين [^95_6].
3. **إضافة Error Code table**.\
وجود أخطاء ثابتة مثل UnsupportedVersion وAuthFailed وIntegrityFailed وReplayDetected وPolicyDenied وSessionExpired سيجعل التوافق والتصحيح أسهل [^95_5][^95_2].
4. **تحديد عناصر integrity/authentication أكثر دقة**.\
ينبغي توضيح ما إذا كانت الرسالة تستخدم signature أو AEAD tag أو كلاهما، وعلى أي طبقة يتم التحقق [^95_2][^95_7].
5. **إضافة compatibility rules for relay envelope**.\
يجب النص صراحة على أن relay لا يشارك في أي version selection داخل payload نفسه، وأن envelope مستقل عن cryptographic session version [^95_2][^95_7].

### النسخة المقترحة للإضافة

أقترح أن تضيف إلى `07-protocol-spec.md` هذا القسم:

```markdown
## 4. Version Negotiation
- Each peer shall advertise its supported protocol versions during handshake.
- The selected version shall be fixed for the lifetime of the session.
- If no compatible version exists, the handshake shall fail safely.
- Downgrade to a weaker version shall be rejected unless explicitly approved by policy.

## 5. State Transitions
| From | To | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| Init | Hello | Start handshake |
| Hello | Challenge | Identity exchange accepted |
| Challenge | Verified | Authentication succeeds |
| Verified | Active | Session keys established |
| Active | Rekey | Session renewal required |
| Active | Revoked | Trust revoked |
| Any | Error | Validation failure |

## 6. Error Codes
- UnsupportedVersion
- AuthenticationFailed
- IntegrityFailed
- ReplayDetected
- SessionExpired
- PolicyDenied
- RevokedIdentity
- RelayFailure
```


### الخلاصة

الملف **صالح كأساس** لكنه سيصبح أفضل بكثير إذا أضفت: version negotiation صريح، state transition table، وerror code table [^95_2][^95_7][^95_6].

## مسودات كود/هيكل 10-ai-layer.md

بما أنك طلبت “تجهيز مسودات الأكواد”، فأفضل شيء هنا هو **هيكل طبقي آمن** يعرّف حدود الـ AI قبل أي تنفيذ فعلي.\
الفكرة الأساسية: AI لا يلمس المفاتيح، لا ينفذ أوامر حساسة، ولا يتجاوز human approval [^95_3][^95_8].

### 1) هيكل ملفات مقترح

```text
src/ai/
  __init__.py
  policy.py
  redaction.py
  prompts.py
  safety.py
  service.py
  events.py
tests/ai/
  test_policy.py
  test_redaction.py
  test_safety.py
```


### 2) `policy.py`

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AIPolicy:
    allow_summaries: bool = True
    allow_classification: bool = True
    allow_anomaly_hints: bool = True
    allow_sensitive_input: bool = False
    allow_key_access: bool = False
    require_human_approval_for_actions: bool = True
```


### 3) `redaction.py`

```python
import re

SECRET_PATTERNS = [
    r"-----BEGIN [A-Z ]+PRIVATE KEY-----.*?-----END [A-Z ]+PRIVATE KEY-----",
    r"(?i)\b(api[_-]?key|secret|token|password)\b\s*[:=]\s*\S+",
]

def redact_sensitive(text: str) -> str:
    redacted = text
    for pattern in SECRET_PATTERNS:
        redacted = re.sub(pattern, "[REDACTED]", redacted, flags=re.DOTALL)
    return redacted
```


### 4) `prompts.py`

```python
SYSTEM_PROMPT = """
You are an assistant for documentation and non-sensitive analysis only.
Do not request, reveal, or process private keys, secrets, or credentials.
Do not make final security decisions.
Return concise, policy-compliant outputs only.
"""
```


### 5) `safety.py`

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass
class AISafetyResult:
    allowed: bool
    reason: str = ""

def validate_ai_request(request_type: str, contains_sensitive_data: bool) -> AISafetyResult:
    blocked = {"key_management", "secret_extraction", "deployment_command", "security_override"}
    if request_type in blocked:
        return AISafetyResult(False, "Blocked request type")
    if contains_sensitive_data:
        return AISafetyResult(False, "Sensitive data not allowed")
    return AISafetyResult(True, "Allowed")
```


### 6) `service.py`

```python
from .policy import AIPolicy
from .redaction import redact_sensitive
from .safety import validate_ai_request

class AIService:
    def __init__(self, policy: AIPolicy):
        self.policy = policy

    def summarize(self, text: str) -> str:
        safe_text = redact_sensitive(text)
        result = validate_ai_request("summary", contains_sensitive_data=False)
        if not result.allowed:
            raise PermissionError(result.reason)
        return safe_text[:500]

    def classify(self, text: str) -> str:
        safe_text = redact_sensitive(text)
        result = validate_ai_request("classification", contains_sensitive_data=False)
        if not result.allowed:
            raise PermissionError(result.reason)
        return "non_sensitive_classification"
```


### 7) `events.py`

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime

@dataclass
class AIEvent:
    event_type: str
    actor: str
    timestamp: datetime
    summary: str
    approved: bool
```


### 8) اختبارات مبدئية

```python
def test_redaction_blocks_private_key():
    text = "-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----abc-----END PRIVATE KEY-----"
    assert "[REDACTED]" in redact_sensitive(text)

def test_block_key_management():
    result = validate_ai_request("key_management", False)
    assert not result.allowed

def test_allow_summary_non_sensitive():
    result = validate_ai_request("summary", False)
    assert result.allowed
```


______________________________________________________________________

## كيف ينعكس هذا داخل 10-ai-layer.md

أقترح أن تجعل الوثيقة الفنية تنص صراحة على:

- AI يشتغل فقط على **non-sensitive inputs**.
- كل input حساس يُمرَّر عبر **redaction**.
- أي action مؤثر يحتاج **human approval**.
- لا يوجد API يسمح بالوصول إلى المفاتيح.
- كل event يسجل في audit log.
- يوجد اختبار أمني إلزامي للـ prompt injection والـ leakage [^95_3][^95_8].


## الخلاصة

- `README.md` صار جاهزًا لربط المستخدم البشري بـ `llms.txt`.
- `07-protocol-spec.md` جيد لكن يحتاج version negotiation وstate/error tables بشكل أوضح [^95_5][^95_7][^95_6].
- `10-ai-layer.md` يحتاج تحويله إلى **policy + redaction + safety gate + service skeleton + tests** حتى يصبح قابلًا للتنفيذ بأمان [^95_3][^95_8].

إذا أردت، أستطيع في الرسالة التالية أن أكتب لك:

1. **نسخة محسّنة كاملة من 07-protocol-spec.md**
2. **نسخة كاملة من 10-ai-layer.md**
3. **قالب test cases لهذين الملفين**

# 07-protocol-spec.md

```markdown
# Protocol Specification

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the protocol behavior for PrivateMesh / النسر, including identity exchange, version negotiation, session establishment, message protection, rekeying, revocation, relay encapsulation, and error handling.

## 2. Scope
This specification covers:
- Protocol versioning.
- Capability discovery.
- Handshake and authentication.
- Secure session creation.
- Encrypted message delivery.
- Replay protection.
- Rekeying and revocation.
- Relay envelope behavior.
- Error codes and failure states.

## 3. Protocol Goals
- Provide authenticated secure communication.
- Protect confidentiality and integrity.
- Prevent replay and downgrade attacks.
- Keep protocol behavior deterministic.
- Support future evolution through version negotiation.
- Keep relay nodes blind to message plaintext.

## 4. Normative Terms
The terms "shall", "must", "should", and "may" are used in the RFC-style normative sense.

## 5. Message Model
Every protocol message shall be represented as a structured object with the following fields:
- Version.
- Message type.
- Sender ID.
- Receiver ID.
- Session ID.
- Timestamp.
- Nonce.
- Sequence number.
- Capability set where applicable.
- Payload.
- Integrity data.
- Authentication data.

## 6. Message Types
- Identity announcement.
- Hello.
- Challenge.
- Response.
- Verification result.
- Encrypted data message.
- Acknowledgment.
- Retry.
- Error.
- Rekey.
- Revocation notice.
- Relay envelope.

## 7. Version Negotiation
### 7.1 Requirements
- Each peer shall advertise its supported protocol versions during handshake.
- The selected version shall be fixed for the lifetime of the session.
- If no compatible version exists, the handshake shall fail safely.
- Downgrade to a weaker version shall be rejected unless explicitly approved by policy.
- Version selection shall be auditable.

### 7.2 Behavior
1. Peer A sends supported versions and capabilities.
2. Peer B replies with supported versions and capabilities.
3. The protocol selects the highest mutually supported version allowed by policy.
4. If policy rejects the candidate version, the handshake fails.
5. The selected version becomes immutable for that session.

## 8. Capability Discovery
Capabilities may include:
- Session rekey support.
- File transfer support.
- Relay envelope support.
- Mesh-ready routing support.
- AI-assisted diagnostic support where allowed.

Capabilities shall be treated as advisory until validated by the session policy.

## 9. Session States
### 9.1 State List
- Init.
- Hello.
- Challenge.
- Verified.
- Active.
- Rekey.
- Revoked.
- Error.

### 9.2 State Transition Table
| From | To | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| Init | Hello | Handshake starts |
| Hello | Challenge | Identity exchange accepted |
| Challenge | Verified | Authentication succeeds |
| Verified | Active | Session keys established |
| Active | Rekey | Renewal required |
| Active | Revoked | Trust revoked |
| Any | Error | Validation failure |

### 9.3 State Rules
- The system shall not allow message exchange in Init, Hello, or Challenge states.
- The system shall only allow encrypted data in Active or Rekey states where policy permits.
- Revoked sessions shall not resume without a new approved handshake.
- Error state shall require explicit recovery or teardown.

## 10. Handshake Flow
1. Peers exchange identity and version information.
2. The protocol validates compatibility and policy constraints.
3. A challenge-response exchange proves possession of identity material.
4. The session manager derives session keys.
5. The session transitions to Active.
6. Any failure terminates the handshake safely.

## 11. Message Protection
### 11.1 Confidentiality
Payloads shall be encrypted before transmission.

### 11.2 Integrity
The recipient shall verify integrity before processing payloads.

### 11.3 Authenticity
Message origin shall be bound to the session or identity context.

### 11.4 Freshness
Messages shall include freshness protection using at least one of:
- nonce,
- timestamp,
- sequence number,
- session binding.

### 11.5 Replay Prevention
The protocol shall reject:
- reused nonces,
- stale timestamps,
- invalid sequence progression,
- repeated session-bound messages outside policy.

## 12. Relay Envelope
### 12.1 Requirements
- Relay nodes shall not need plaintext.
- The relay envelope shall be distinct from payload encryption.
- Relay metadata shall be minimized.
- Relay processing shall not alter protocol trust semantics.

### 12.2 Behavior
1. The sender creates an encrypted payload.
2. The payload is wrapped in a relay envelope when needed.
3. The relay forwards the envelope without inspecting the payload.
4. The recipient unwraps and processes the payload locally.

## 13. Rekeying
### 13.1 Requirements
- Sessions shall support renewal or rekeying.
- Rekeying shall not expose secret material unnecessarily.
- Rekeying shall preserve continuity where policy allows.
- Rekeying events shall be auditable.

### 13.2 Triggers
Rekeying may occur when:
- a session lifetime limit is reached,
- policy requires it,
- risk changes,
- a compromise is suspected,
- key rotation is scheduled.

## 14. Revocation
### 14.1 Requirements
- Revocation shall immediately block trust use of a session or identity.
- Revocation notices shall be verifiable.
- Revoked peers shall require new authorization before resuming communication.

### 14.2 Behavior
1. The system records revocation.
2. Active sessions are marked invalid.
3. Dependent trust artifacts are refreshed if needed.
4. The event is logged for review.

## 15. Error Handling
### 15.1 Error Codes
- UnsupportedVersion.
- AuthenticationFailed.
- IntegrityFailed.
- ReplayDetected.
- SessionExpired.
- PolicyDenied.
- RevokedIdentity.
- RelayFailure.
- MalformedMessage.
- CapabilityMismatch.

### 15.2 Error Rules
- Errors shall be deterministic.
- Errors shall not leak secret material.
- Errors shall be suitable for logging and incident triage.
- Unknown message types shall be rejected unless explicitly allowed by policy.

## 16. Parsing and Validation
- Parsing shall be strict.
- Unknown required fields shall cause failure.
- Ambiguous fields shall cause failure.
- Validation shall happen before decryption where possible, and before delivery always.
- Message sizes shall be bounded.

## 17. Compatibility Rules
- Backward compatibility shall be explicit.
- Unsafe fallback behavior shall be rejected by default.
- Compatibility exceptions shall require policy and documentation updates.
- New major versions shall require updated ADRs and tests.

## 18. Security Considerations
- Do not trust transport.
- Do not trust relay nodes with content.
- Do not accept unauthenticated or malformed structures.
- Do not silently downgrade security.
- Do not use error messages to reveal secrets or internals.

## 19. Compliance Notes
This document is intended to be implemented alongside the architecture, threat model, key management plan, and traceability matrix. Any protocol change must be reflected in those artifacts.

## 20. Summary
The protocol is explicit, versioned, authenticated, and designed to resist replay and downgrade attacks while keeping relays blind to content.
```

# 10-ai-layer.md

````markdown
# AI Layer

## 1. Purpose
This document defines the safe scope, technical boundaries, operational controls, and implementation rules for AI-assisted features in PrivateMesh / النسر.

## 2. Scope
The AI layer may be used for:
- documentation assistance,
- non-sensitive summarization,
- classification of non-sensitive operational events,
- anomaly hints for human review,
- consistency checks across documentation.

The AI layer shall not be used for:
- private key handling,
- security decision authority,
- deployment authority,
- trust assignment,
- secret extraction,
- uncontrolled data access.

## 3. Design Goals
- Keep AI bounded and non-authoritative.
- Prevent secret leakage.
- Require redaction where needed.
- Preserve human approval for sensitive actions.
- Log AI usage for audit.
- Make AI behavior testable and reviewable.

## 4. Safety Principles
- AI is an assistant, not a controller.
- Human approval is required for any security-relevant action.
- Sensitive data shall be redacted before AI processing whenever possible.
- AI shall not have direct access to private keys or secret stores.
- AI shall not bypass access control.
- AI outputs shall be treated as advisory.

## 5. Allowed Functions
### 5.1 Documentation Support
- Draft or improve documentation text.
- Summarize non-sensitive docs.
- Suggest structure improvements.
- Detect missing sections or inconsistent references.

### 5.2 Operational Assistance
- Summarize non-sensitive log trends.
- Classify benign operational events.
- Highlight unusual patterns for human review.

### 5.3 Quality Assistance
- Suggest test coverage gaps.
- Detect terminology drift.
- Identify duplicate or conflicting documentation references.

## 6. Prohibited Functions
- Reading raw private keys.
- Modifying trust state automatically.
- Executing deployment commands.
- Overriding security policy.
- Exporting secrets.
- Making final allow/deny decisions for sensitive actions.
- Accessing production data without explicit approval.

## 7. Data Handling Rules
### 7.1 Intake
AI requests shall be classified before processing.

### 7.2 Redaction
Sensitive text shall be redacted prior to AI input when possible.

### 7.3 Minimization
Only the minimum data needed for the task shall be sent to AI.

### 7.4 Retention
AI prompts and outputs containing sensitive context shall follow the project’s retention rules.

### 7.5 Audit
Each AI use shall emit an audit event with:
- actor,
- purpose,
- input classification,
- approval status,
- timestamp,
- outcome.

## 8. Sensitivity Classification
The system should classify AI inputs into:
- Public.
- Internal.
- Sensitive.
- Secret.
- Restricted.

Rules:
- Public and internal data may be eligible for routine summarization.
- Sensitive data requires redaction or explicit approval.
- Secret and restricted data shall not be sent to AI unless the design explicitly permits a safe transformed version.

## 9. Redaction Requirements
Redaction shall detect and remove or mask:
- private keys,
- API keys,
- tokens,
- passwords,
- secrets,
- personal identifiers where policy requires,
- internal endpoints where necessary,
- unapproved operational details.

### 9.1 Redaction Workflow
1. Input is classified.
2. Sensitive fragments are detected.
3. Fragments are masked or removed.
4. A safe copy is passed to AI.
5. The output is reviewed if needed.

## 10. Approval Model
### 10.1 No Approval Needed
- Non-sensitive documentation summarization.
- Formatting suggestions for public docs.

### 10.2 Approval Required
- Any action that affects production.
- Any action that touches secrets.
- Any classification decision that changes trust.
- Any AI-generated recommendation used to modify security controls.

## 11. Technical Architecture
### 11.1 Components
- AI policy engine.
- Redaction engine.
- Prompt builder.
- Safety gate.
- Audit logger.
- Human approval workflow.
- AI service adapter.

### 11.2 Flow
1. A request enters the AI layer.
2. The policy engine classifies the request.
3. The redaction engine removes sensitive content if needed.
4. The safety gate validates the request.
5. The prompt builder prepares a safe prompt.
6. The AI service generates an advisory response.
7. The audit logger records the event.
8. Human review occurs when required.

## 12. Safety Gate Rules
The safety gate shall deny requests that:
- request key access,
- request secret extraction,
- request deployment execution,
- request security override,
- contain prohibited sensitive content,
- violate environment policy,
- bypass approval.

## 13. Logging and Monitoring
Logs shall record:
- request type,
- classification,
- redaction status,
- approval status,
- response status,
- error codes,
- actor and environment.

Logs shall not include secret material or full sensitive payloads.

## 14. Implementation Guidance
The AI layer should be implemented as a separate module with strict interfaces and no direct access to secret stores.

### 14.1 Suggested Module Layout
```text
src/ai/
  __init__.py
  policy.py
  redaction.py
  prompts.py
  safety.py
  service.py
  events.py
tests/ai/
  test_policy.py
  test_redaction.py
  test_safety.py
  test_service.py
````


### 14.2 Suggested Data Flow Contracts

- Inputs must be classified.
- Redacted data should be passed downstream.
- Output should be tagged as advisory.
- Sensitive outputs should trigger review if needed.


## 15. Reference Implementation Sketch

### 15.1 policy.py

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AIPolicy:
    allow_summaries: bool = True
    allow_classification: bool = True
    allow_anomaly_hints: bool = True
    allow_sensitive_input: bool = False
    allow_key_access: bool = False
    require_human_approval_for_actions: bool = True
```


### 15.2 redaction.py

```python
import re

SECRET_PATTERNS = [
    r"-----BEGIN [A-Z ]+PRIVATE KEY-----.*?-----END [A-Z ]+PRIVATE KEY-----",
    r"(?i)\b(api[_-]?key|secret|token|password)\b\s*[:=]\s*\S+",
]

def redact_sensitive(text: str) -> str:
    redacted = text
    for pattern in SECRET_PATTERNS:
        redacted = re.sub(pattern, "[REDACTED]", redacted, flags=re.DOTALL)
    return redacted
```


### 15.3 prompts.py

```python
SYSTEM_PROMPT = """
You are an assistant for documentation and non-sensitive analysis only.
Do not request, reveal, or process private keys, secrets, or credentials.
Do not make final security decisions.
Return concise, policy-compliant outputs only.
"""
```


### 15.4 safety.py

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass
class AISafetyResult:
    allowed: bool
    reason: str = ""

def validate_ai_request(request_type: str, contains_sensitive_data: bool) -> AISafetyResult:
    blocked = {"key_management", "secret_extraction", "deployment_command", "security_override"}
    if request_type in blocked:
        return AISafetyResult(False, "Blocked request type")
    if contains_sensitive_data:
        return AISafetyResult(False, "Sensitive data not allowed")
    return AISafetyResult(True, "Allowed")
```


### 15.5 service.py

```python
from .policy import AIPolicy
from .redaction import redact_sensitive
from .safety import validate_ai_request

class AIService:
    def __init__(self, policy: AIPolicy):
        self.policy = policy

    def summarize(self, text: str) -> str:
        safe_text = redact_sensitive(text)
        result = validate_ai_request("summary", contains_sensitive_data=False)
        if not result.allowed:
            raise PermissionError(result.reason)
        return safe_text[:500]

    def classify(self, text: str) -> str:
        safe_text = redact_sensitive(text)
        result = validate_ai_request("classification", contains_sensitive_data=False)
        if not result.allowed:
            raise PermissionError(result.reason)
        return "non_sensitive_classification"
```


### 15.6 events.py

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime

@dataclass
class AIEvent:
    event_type: str
    actor: str
    timestamp: datetime
    summary: str
    approved: bool
```


## 16. Operational Rules

- AI must not run with elevated privileges by default.
- AI must not write directly to production resources.
- AI-generated recommendations for security-sensitive changes require review.
- AI use should be observable and bounded.
- AI output should be treated as untrusted until validated.


## 17. Testing Requirements

The AI layer shall be tested for:

- redaction correctness,
- blocked request types,
- approval enforcement,
- prompt injection resistance,
- logging completeness,
- environment separation,
- no key exposure,
- safe fallback behavior.


## 18. Failure Modes

The following failures must be handled safely:

- redaction misses,
- prompt injection,
- data overexposure,
- unauthorized action requests,
- unsafe output generation,
- logging leakage.


## 19. Security Considerations

- Redaction is mandatory for sensitive content.
- Secrets must never be routed to the AI layer.
- Human review is mandatory for security-impacting actions.
- The AI layer must remain isolated from secrets and trust controls.
- Model outputs should never be treated as authoritative security evidence.


## 20. Summary

The AI layer is strictly a bounded assistance system. It helps with documentation and non-sensitive analysis, but it must never own secrets, trust, or operational authority.

````

***
````

# tests/protocol/test_protocol_spec.py

```python
def test_version_negotiation_requires_compatibility():
    supported_a = {1, 2}
    supported_b = {2, 3}
    allowed = supported_a.intersection(supported_b)
    assert 2 in allowed

def test_state_transition_table_present():
    states = ["Init", "Hello", "Challenge", "Verified", "Active", "Rekey", "Revoked", "Error"]
    assert "Active" in states
    assert "Error" in states

def test_error_codes_defined():
    errors = {
        "UnsupportedVersion",
        "AuthenticationFailed",
        "IntegrityFailed",
        "ReplayDetected",
        "SessionExpired",
        "PolicyDenied",
        "RevokedIdentity",
        "RelayFailure",
        "MalformedMessage",
        "CapabilityMismatch",
    }
    assert "ReplayDetected" in errors
```

# docs/appendices/ai-safety-checklist.md

```markdown
# AI Safety Checklist

- [ ] Input classified.
- [ ] Sensitive data redacted.
- [ ] Request type allowed.
- [ ] No keys or secrets included.
- [ ] Human approval obtained if required.
- [ ] Output treated as advisory.
- [ ] Audit event recorded.
- [ ] Logs contain no secrets.
```


______________________________________________________________________

## ما الذي تم تطبيقه هنا

- `07-protocol-spec.md` أصبح أكثر صرامة من ناحية **version negotiation** و**state transitions** و**error codes**، وهو ما ينسجم مع إرشادات تصميم البروتوكولات التي تؤكد أهمية وصف الحالات والانتقالات والأخطاء [^96_4][^96_5].
- `10-ai-layer.md` أصبح وثيقة سياسة وتشغيل وهندسة معًا، مع redaction وapproval gates وعزل واضح للمخاطر، وهو ما يتوافق مع إرشادات الأمان في أنظمة AI والردع من تسرب البيانات [^96_6][^96_7][^96_8].
- اختبارات البروتوكول وAI أصبحت قابلة للبناء مباشرة كأساس لمجموعة اختبار أولية.


## الخطوة التالية المنطقية

أستطيع الآن أن أكتب لك مباشرة:

1. `README.md` النهائي مع قسم quick start وstructure.
2. `docs/appendices/docs-index.md` النهائي بصيغة محدثة.
3. `setup` أو `Makefile` أولي يهيئ هيكل المشروع والاختبارات.

[^96_1]: https://llmstxt.org/

# تابع تغطيه الكل كامل

أكيد. هذا هو **إغلاق الحزمة بالكامل**: بنية الجذر، README النهائي، `llms.txt` الصحيح، مراجعة نهائية لتوافق البروتوكول، ومجموعة مسودات AI منظمة يمكن تنفيذها مباشرة [^97_1][^97_2][^97_3].\
كما سأضيف لك **ملف فهرس المجلدات** و**قالب تشغيل أولي** حتى تصبح الحزمة قابلة للبدء والتنقل والاستخدام الآلي والبشري من أول يوم [^97_4][^97_5][^97_6].
